<a id='question-079'></a>

### Question 079: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 29 (complex,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying chronic allograft rejection, specifically the role of non-HLA antigens, antibody-mediated injury, and fibrosis, distinguishing it from acute rejection and hyperacute rejection. (理解慢性移植物排斥的免疫机制，特别是非HLA抗原、抗体介导的损伤和纤维化的作用，将其与急性排斥和超急性排斥区分开。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with a 6/6 HLA match. The patient is started on standard immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. The initial post-transplant course is uneventful, with stable graft function and normal serum creatinine levels for the first 6 months. However, over the next 18 months, the patient experiences a gradual decline in graft function, evidenced by a progressive increase in serum creatinine from 1.2 mg/dL to 3.5 mg/dL. A renal biopsy performed at 24 months post-transplant reveals interstitial fibrosis, tubular atrophy, and glomerulopathy characterized by transplant glomerulopathy (C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries and glomeruli, but no evidence of acute cellular rejection or significant antibody-mediated rejection features like endothelialitis). Donor-specific antibodies (DSA) are detected in the patient's serum, targeting non-HLA antigens, specifically MICA and angiotensin II type 1 receptor (AT1R). The patient's immunosuppression regimen is optimized, but graft function continues to deteriorate, eventually leading to graft failure 3 years post-transplant.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the chronic allograft dysfunction observed in this patient?

(A) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity mediated by pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies leading to hyperacute rejection.
(B) T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against donor MHC class I molecules causing acute cellular rejection.
(C) Antibody-mediated injury targeting non-HLA antigens, leading to endothelial activation, inflammation, and subsequent fibrosis.
(D) Direct viral infection of the renal allograft causing cytopathic effects and inflammation.
(E) Recurrence of the original disease (diabetic nephropathy) in the transplanted kidney.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 58-year-old male, ESRD due to diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. (患者资料：58岁男性，因糖尿病肾病导致ESRD，接受肾脏移植。)
- **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor, 6/6 HLA match, standard immunosuppression (tacrolimus, MMF, prednisone). (移植细节：尸体捐献，HLA 6/6配型，标准免疫抑制。)
- **Timeline:** Initial 6 months uneventful, followed by gradual decline in graft function over 18 months (serum creatinine increase from 1.2 to 3.5 mg/dL). (时间线：最初6个月无事，随后18个月内逐渐下降，血肌酐升高。)
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial fibrosis, tubular atrophy, transplant glomerulopathy (C4d deposition, no acute cellular rejection or significant endothelialitis). (活检结果：间质纤维化、管间质萎缩、移植性肾小球病（C4d沉积，无急性细胞性排斥或显著内皮炎）。)
- **Serology:** Presence of donor-specific antibodies (DSA) targeting non-HLA antigens (MICA, AT1R). (血清学：存在针对非HLA抗原（MICA、AT1R）的供体特异性抗体（DSA）。)
- **Outcome:** Progressive graft failure despite optimized immunosuppression. (结果：尽管优化了免疫抑制，但移植物逐渐衰竭。)
- **Key Clues:** Gradual decline over months/years, biopsy showing fibrosis/atrophy/transplant glomerulopathy (C4d+), presence of non-HLA DSA, lack of acute rejection features. This points towards chronic antibody-mediated rejection (CAMR). (关键线索：数月/数年内的逐渐下降，活检显示纤维化/萎缩/移植性肾小球病（C4d+），存在非HLA DSA，缺乏急性排斥特征。这指向慢性抗体介导的排斥（CAMR）。)

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Antibody-Mediated Rejection (CAMR):** This is a major cause of late allograft loss. It involves the development of DSAs, often against non-HLA antigens like MICA (MHC class I chain-related protein A) and AT1R, in addition to HLA antigens. (慢性抗体介导的排斥（CAMR）是晚期移植物丢失的主要原因。它涉及供体特异性抗体（DSA）的产生，通常针对非HLA抗原，如MICA和AT1R，以及HLA抗原。)
- **Mechanism:** These antibodies bind to endothelial cells in the graft vasculature, activating complement (leading to C4d deposition) and Fc receptors on endothelial cells and inflammatory cells. This triggers endothelial activation, inflammation, microvascular injury (glomerulopathy, peritubular capillaritis), and ultimately leads to chronic parenchymal damage (fibrosis, tubular atrophy). (机制：这些抗体结合移植物血管内皮细胞，激活补体（导致C4d沉积）和内皮细胞及炎症细胞上的Fc受体。这会触发内皮激活、炎症、微血管损伤（肾小球病、管周毛细血管炎），并最终导致慢性实质性损伤（纤维化、管间质萎缩）。)
- **Non-HLA Antigens:** MICA is upregulated on endothelial cells during inflammation and stress, making it a target for DSA. AT1R is involved in vasoconstriction and fibrosis, and antibodies against it can contribute to graft dysfunction. (非HLA抗原：MICA在炎症和应激期间在内皮细胞上表达上调，使其成为DSA的目标。AT1R参与血管收缩和纤维化，针对它的抗体可能导致移植物功能障碍。)
- **Distinction from Acute Rejection:** CAMR develops slowly over months to years, unlike acute rejection which occurs within days to weeks. Biopsy findings differ; CAMR shows fibrosis, atrophy, and transplant glomerulopathy (C4d deposition), while acute cellular rejection shows T-cell infiltrates and acute tubular injury, and acute antibody-mediated rejection shows prominent endothelialitis and C4d deposition. (与急性排斥的区别：CAMR发展缓慢，数月至数年，而急性排斥发生在数天至数周内。活检结果不同；CAMR显示纤维化、萎缩和移植性肾小球病（C4d沉积），而急性细胞性排斥显示T细胞浸润和急性管间质损伤，而急性抗体介导的排斥显示显著的内皮炎和C4d沉积。)
- **Distinction from Hyperacute Rejection:** Hyperacute rejection occurs within minutes to hours due to pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies (often ABO or HLA) causing rapid complement activation and thrombosis. This patient had a 6/6 HLA match and a gradual onset, ruling out hyperacute rejection. (与超急性排斥的区别：超急性排斥发生在数分钟至数小时内，这是由于预先存在的抗HLA抗体（通常是ABO或HLA）导致快速的补体激活和血栓形成。该患者HLA 6/6配型且发病逐渐，排除了超急性排斥。)

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Complement-dependent cytotoxicity mediated by pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies leading to hyperacute rejection.** This is incorrect. Hyperacute rejection occurs immediately (minutes to hours) due to pre-existing antibodies, typically ABO or HLA, causing widespread thrombosis. This patient had a 6/6 HLA match and a gradual onset over months/years, inconsistent with hyperacute rejection. The biopsy findings (fibrosis, atrophy, transplant glomerulopathy) are also characteristic of chronic rejection, not hyperacute rejection. (错误。超急性排斥发生在立即（数分钟至数小时内），这是由于预先存在的抗体（通常是ABO或HLA）导致广泛的血栓形成。该患者HLA 6/6配型且发病逐渐，与超急性排斥不符。活检结果（纤维化、萎缩、移植性肾小球病）也具有慢性排斥的特征，而不是超急性排斥。)
- (B) **T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against donor MHC class I molecules causing acute cellular rejection.** This is incorrect. Acute cellular rejection typically occurs within the first few weeks/months post-transplant and is characterized by T-cell infiltrates causing acute tubular injury. The biopsy findings in this patient (fibrosis, atrophy, transplant glomerulopathy, C4d deposition) and the gradual onset over 18 months are not typical of acute cellular rejection. While T-cells play a role in chronic rejection, the primary driver described here is antibody-mediated injury against non-HLA antigens. (错误。急性细胞性排斥通常发生在移植后的最初几周/几个月内，其特征是T细胞浸润导致急性管间质损伤。该患者的活检结果（纤维化、萎缩、移植性肾小球病、C4d沉积）和18个月内的逐渐发生与急性细胞性排斥不符。虽然T细胞在慢性排斥中起作用，但此处描述的主要驱动因素是针对非HLA抗原的抗体介导的损伤。)
- (D) **Direct viral infection of the renal allograft causing cytopathic effects and inflammation.** This is incorrect. While viral infections (e.g., BK virus, CMV) can cause allograft dysfunction, they typically present with specific cytopathic effects (e.g., viral inclusions) and often occur in the context of more intense immunosuppression. The biopsy findings described (fibrosis, atrophy, transplant glomerulopathy, C4d deposition) and the presence of DSA targeting non-HLA antigens are not characteristic of viral infection. (错误。虽然病毒感染（例如BK病毒、CMV）会导致移植物功能障碍，但它们通常表现出特定的细胞致病效应（例如病毒包涵体），并且通常发生在更强免疫抑制的背景下。所描述的活检结果（纤维化、萎缩、移植性肾小球病、C4d沉积）和针对非HLA抗原的DSA的存在不是病毒感染的特征。)
- (E) **Recurrence of the original disease (diabetic nephropathy) in the transplanted kidney.** This is incorrect. While recurrence of the original disease is a known complication in kidney transplantation (especially for FSGS and IgA nephropathy), diabetic nephropathy typically develops slowly over many years, even in the native kidneys. The rapid decline in graft function over 18 months, coupled with the specific biopsy findings (transplant glomerulopathy, C4d deposition) and the presence of DSA, strongly points towards rejection rather than disease recurrence. Furthermore, the biopsy showed *transplant* glomerulopathy, a specific lesion associated with antibody-mediated rejection, not typical diabetic nephropathy changes. (错误。虽然原发病的复发是肾移植的已知并发症（尤其是FSGS和IgA肾病），但糖尿病肾病通常在数年内缓慢发展，即使是在原生肾脏中。18个月内移植物功能迅速下降，加上特定的活检结果（移植性肾小球病、C4d沉积）和DSA的存在，强烈提示排斥反应而不是疾病复发。此外，活检显示*移植性*肾小球病，这是一种与抗体介导的排斥相关的特定病变，而不是典型的糖尿病肾病变化。)

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature | Chronic Antibody-Mediated Rejection (CAMR) | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Hyperacute Rejection (HAR) | Disease Recurrence | Viral Infection |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Onset** | Months to years | Days to weeks | Minutes to hours | Variable, often years | Variable |
| **Primary Mediator** | Antibodies (DSA, often non-HLA) | T-cells | Pre-formed antibodies (HLA/ABO) | Underlying disease process | Virus |
| **Key Biopsy Findings** | Fibrosis, atrophy, transplant glomerulopathy, C4d deposition | T-cell infiltrates, acute tubular injury | Widespread thrombosis, endothelial damage | Features of original disease | Viral inclusions, cytopathic effects |
| **DSA Presence** | Common, often targeting non-HLA | Uncommon | Not applicable (pre-formed) | Uncommon | Uncommon |
| **Clinical Course** | Gradual decline in function | Acute decline in function | Immediate graft loss | Gradual or acute decline | Variable |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Chronic antibody-mediated rejection (CAMR) is a major cause of late allograft failure, often driven by DSAs targeting non-HLA antigens like MICA and AT1R. It is characterized by gradual graft dysfunction, biopsy findings of fibrosis, tubular atrophy, and transplant glomerulopathy (C4d deposition), and the presence of DSAs. Differentiate CAMR from acute rejection (T-cell mediated, rapid onset, T-cell infiltrates), hyperacute rejection (pre-formed antibodies, immediate thrombosis), disease recurrence (features of original disease), and viral infection (viral inclusions).

<a id='question-080'></a>

### Question 080: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 30 (expressed)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular mechanisms underlying the rejection of transplanted organs, specifically focusing on the role of complement activation and the C4d deposition as a marker of antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) in kidney transplantation. (理解器官移植排斥机制，特别是抗体介导的排斥 (AMR) 中补体激活和 C4d 沉积在肾脏移植中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) screen was negative. He is started on standard immunosuppression consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Six months post-transplant, he presents with a gradual decline in serum creatinine from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 2.5 mg/dL, accompanied by mild proteinuria (1 g/day). A renal biopsy is performed. Light microscopy shows mild interstitial edema and focal tubular atrophy. Immunofluorescence microscopy reveals linear deposition of C3 and IgG along the glomerular basement membrane (GBM) and peritubular capillaries. Electron microscopy shows subendothelial immune complex deposits. A biopsy sample is stained for C4d. The C4d stain shows strong, diffuse positivity in the peritubular capillaries and segmental positivity in the glomeruli. Which of the following mechanisms is most directly responsible for the observed C4d deposition in this patient's kidney graft?

(A) Activation of the alternative complement pathway by bacterial endotoxins contaminating the graft.
(B) Activation of the classical complement pathway by donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) binding to HLA antigens on the graft endothelium.
(C) Activation of the lectin pathway of complement by mannose-binding lectin (MBL) binding to microbial components.
(D) Deposition of C4d due to passive transfer of pre-formed C4d-containing immune complexes from the donor.
(E) Activation of the intrinsic coagulation pathway leading to thrombin generation and subsequent complement activation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential complications post-transplant.
- **Timeline:** 6 months post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for chronic rejection or antibody-mediated rejection (AMR).
- **Clinical Presentation:** Gradual rise in creatinine (from 1.2 to 2.5 mg/dL) and mild proteinuria (1 g/day). These indicate graft dysfunction.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). Despite immunosuppression, rejection can still occur.
- **Pre-transplant PRA:** Negative. This suggests no pre-existing antibodies against donor HLA antigens, but DSAs can develop post-transplant.
- **Renal Biopsy Findings:**
    - **Light Microscopy:** Mild interstitial edema and focal tubular atrophy. Non-specific findings, but can be seen in AMR.
    - **Immunofluorescence:** Linear C3 and IgG deposition along GBM and peritubular capillaries. This pattern is highly suggestive of antibody-mediated injury. Linear deposition along the GBM is characteristic of anti-GBM disease, but the peritubular capillary involvement points towards AMR.
    - **Electron Microscopy:** Subendothelial immune complex deposits. Confirms immune complex deposition, consistent with AMR.
    - **C4d Staining:** Strong, diffuse positivity in peritubular capillaries and segmental positivity in glomeruli. C4d is a stable breakdown product of C4b, which is part of the classical and lectin complement pathways. Its deposition in microvasculature is a hallmark of *active* complement activation, strongly indicating AMR.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism *most directly responsible* for the observed C4d deposition.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Occurs when recipient antibodies (often DSAs) target donor HLA antigens expressed on the graft endothelium.
- **Complement Activation:** DSAs binding to HLA antigens activate the classical complement pathway.
- **Classical Pathway:** Initiated by the C1 complex (C1q, C1r, C1s) binding to the Fc portion of IgG or IgM antibodies bound to antigen. This leads to the cleavage of C4 into C4a and C4b, and C2 into C2a and C2b. C4b and C2a combine to form the C3 convertase (C4b2a).
- **C3 Convertase:** Cleaves C3 into C3a and C3b. C3b opsonizes pathogens and cells, and binds to the C3 convertase to form the C5 convertase (C4b2a3b).
- **C5 Convertase:** Cleaves C5 into C5a and C5b. C5b initiates the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC, C5b-9), which damages endothelial cells.
- **C4d:** C4b is rapidly cleaved into C4a and C4b. C4b is further cleaved into C4c and C4d. C4d is a stable fragment that remains bound to the endothelial surface, serving as a marker of recent classical (and lectin) pathway activation. Its deposition in peritubular capillaries is a key diagnostic criterion for AMR in kidney biopsies.
- **In this case:** The linear IgG/C3 deposition and subendothelial immune complexes, along with the strong C4d staining, strongly point to AMR driven by DSAs activating the classical complement pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Activation of the alternative complement pathway by bacterial endotoxins contaminating the graft.** The alternative pathway is typically activated by microbial surfaces or abnormal host cells. While contamination is possible, the *linear* IgG/C3 deposition and the specific pattern of C4d staining (peritubular capillaries) are much more characteristic of antibody-mediated classical pathway activation by DSAs targeting HLA. Alternative pathway activation would typically lead to C3 deposition without significant C4d deposition (as C4 is not involved) or would be associated with different patterns of deposition.
- (C) **Activation of the lectin pathway of complement by mannose-binding lectin (MBL) binding to microbial components.** The lectin pathway is activated by MBL or ficolins binding to carbohydrates on microbial surfaces. Similar to the alternative pathway, it does not involve C4 cleavage in the same way as the classical pathway, and would not typically explain the linear IgG/C3 deposition pattern seen here. C4d deposition can occur, but the primary driver in this scenario is clearly antibody-mediated.
- (D) **Deposition of C4d due to passive transfer of pre-formed C4d-containing immune complexes from the donor.** C4d is a relatively unstable molecule. While some C4d might be present in the donor graft, significant deposition in the recipient's microvasculature requires *active* complement activation within the recipient's circulation. Passive transfer would not explain the strong, diffuse C4d staining indicative of ongoing complement activation. Furthermore, the patient was PRA negative pre-transplant, making pre-formed antibodies less likely, although *de novo* DSAs are common.
- (E) **Activation of the intrinsic coagulation pathway leading to thrombin generation and subsequent complement activation.** While coagulation and complement systems interact (e.g., thrombin can activate C5), the primary histological findings (linear IgG/C3, subendothelial deposits, C4d staining) point directly to an immune complex-mediated process involving antibodies and complement activation, specifically the classical pathway. Coagulation activation alone does not typically produce this pattern.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Alternative Pathway Activation | Lectin Pathway Activation | Bacterial Contamination | Coagulation Activation |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :------------------------ | :---------------------- | :--------------------- |
| **Trigger**             | DSAs binding HLA                 | Microbial surfaces, abnormal cells | Microbial carbohydrates  | Bacterial endotoxins    | Thrombin generation    |
| **Complement Pathway**  | Classical                         | Alternative                    | Lectin                    | Alternative/Classical   | Indirect (C5 activation) |
| **C4d Deposition**      | Yes (strong marker)               | No                             | Yes                      | Variable                | Variable               |
| **IgG/C3 Deposition**   | Linear/Granular (GBM, PTC)        | Granular                       | Granular                  | Variable                | Variable               |
| **Key Finding**         | DSAs, C4d in microvasculature     | C3 deposition                  | MBL/Ficolin binding       | Endotoxin detection     | Fibrin deposition      |
| **Relevance to Vignette** | **Directly matches findings**     | Less likely                    | Less likely               | Less likely             | Less likely            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries is a key histological marker of active antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) in kidney transplants, indicating classical (or lectin) complement pathway activation by antibodies (often DSAs) binding to endothelial antigens. Differentiating AMR from T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) or other causes of graft dysfunction relies on recognizing these specific histological patterns. Key differentials include TCMR (interstitial inflammation, tubular injury), chronic allograft nephropathy (tubulointerstitial fibrosis, glomerulosclerosis), and calcineurin inhibitor toxicity (arteriolar hyalinosis).

<a id='question-081'></a>

### Question 081: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 31 (codominant)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Codominant expression of minor histocompatibility antigens in allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (allo-HSCT) leads to graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) when donor T cells recognize recipient antigens as foreign, particularly in the context of HLA matching. (在异基因造血干细胞移植中，次要组织相容性抗原的共显性表达会导致移植物抗宿主病 (GVHD)，当供体 T 细胞将受体抗原识别为异体时，尤其是在 HLA 匹配的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with acute myeloid leukemia (AML) undergoes an allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (allo-HSCT) from a matched unrelated donor. The patient's HLA typing is A*02:01/B*07:02/C*04:01/DRB1*15:01/DQB1*06:02. The donor's HLA typing is A*02:01/B*07:02/C*04:01/DRB1*15:01/DQB1*06:02. However, the donor also expresses the C*04:02 allele, which the recipient does not possess. The patient receives standard conditioning chemotherapy and immunosuppression (cyclosporine and tacrolimus) and is given prophylactic antibiotics and antivirals. Three weeks post-transplant, the patient develops severe diarrhea, jaundice, and a maculopapular rash. Liver biopsy reveals cholestatic jaundice with bile duct injury and apoptosis of hepatocytes. Skin biopsy shows spongiosis and lymphocytic infiltrate. Bone marrow biopsy shows no evidence of AML relapse. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a significant population of donor-derived CD8+ T cells expressing the TCR specific for a minor histocompatibility antigen presented by HLA-C molecules.

Which of the following mechanisms best explains the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Donor T cells recognizing recipient HLA-A molecules as foreign, leading to direct alloreactivity.
(B) Donor T cells recognizing recipient minor histocompatibility antigens presented by HLA-C molecules, leading to indirect alloreactivity.
(C) Recipient B cells producing antibodies against donor HLA-C molecules, leading to antibody-mediated rejection.
(D) Recipient NK cells recognizing and killing donor cells lacking inhibitory signals from recipient HLA-C molecules.
(E) Donor dendritic cells presenting recipient self-antigens to recipient T cells, leading to autoimmune-like liver injury.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male with AML undergoing allo-HSCT. This sets the stage for potential complications like GVHD or rejection.
- **HLA Typing:** Both patient and donor are fully matched for HLA-A, B, DRB1, and DQB1 loci. However, the donor expresses HLA-C*04:02, while the recipient expresses HLA-C*04:01. This difference in a minor histocompatibility antigen (HLA-C) is a crucial clue.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Severe diarrhea, jaundice, maculopapular rash developing 3 weeks post-transplant. This classic triad is highly suggestive of acute GVHD.
- **Diagnostic Findings:**
    - Liver biopsy: Cholestatic jaundice, bile duct injury, hepatocyte apoptosis - characteristic features of GVHD affecting the liver.
    - Skin biopsy: Spongiosis, lymphocytic infiltrate - characteristic features of GVHD affecting the skin.
    - Bone marrow biopsy: No AML relapse - rules out relapse as the cause of symptoms.
    - Flow cytometry: Donor-derived CD8+ T cells specific for a minor histocompatibility antigen presented by HLA-C molecules. This directly implicates donor T cells recognizing recipient antigens presented via HLA-C.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the pathogenesis. The clinical picture points strongly to acute GVHD mediated by donor T cells recognizing recipient antigens. The specific HLA mismatch (HLA-C) and the flow cytometry finding pinpoint the mechanism.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Allogeneic HSCT & GVHD:** In allo-HSCT, the donor immune system (primarily T cells) present in the graft can recognize the recipient's tissues as foreign, leading to GVHD.
- **HLA Matching:** HLA matching aims to minimize alloreactivity. Full HLA matching at HLA-A, B, and DR loci significantly reduces the risk, but mismatches at minor histocompatibility loci (like HLA-C, HLA-E, HLA-G, or polymorphic non-HLA antigens) can still trigger GVHD.
- **Minor Histocompatibility Antigens:** These are polymorphic peptides derived from normal cellular proteins that differ between individuals. They are presented by HLA molecules.
- **HLA-C Presentation:** HLA-C molecules are expressed on most nucleated cells and present peptides to CD8+ T cells. The difference in HLA-C alleles (C*04:01 vs. C*04:02) means the donor T cells can recognize peptides presented by the recipient's HLA-C*04:01 molecules as foreign, or peptides presented by the donor's HLA-C*04:02 molecules that are not present in the recipient. In this case, the flow cytometry confirms donor CD8+ T cells recognizing a minor antigen presented by HLA-C.
- **Direct vs. Indirect Alloreactivity:**
    - **Direct:** Donor T cells directly recognize intact foreign HLA molecules (major or minor) on recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs). This is the primary mechanism for acute GVHD, especially involving minor histocompatibility antigens.
    - **Indirect:** Recipient APCs process and present donor HLA peptides (major or minor) via their own HLA molecules to recipient T cells. This is more characteristic of chronic rejection or chronic GVHD.
- **Mechanism in this Case:** The flow cytometry finding of donor CD8+ T cells recognizing a minor histocompatibility antigen presented by HLA-C molecules is the hallmark of *direct* alloreactivity against minor histocompatibility antigens. The HLA-C mismatch is the specific trigger.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Donor T cells recognizing recipient HLA-A molecules as foreign, leading to direct alloreactivity. Incorrect. The vignette states the patient and donor are matched for HLA-A. While HLA-A mismatches can cause GVHD, this is not the case here.
- (C): Recipient B cells producing antibodies against donor HLA-C molecules, leading to antibody-mediated rejection. Incorrect. This describes antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), which typically involves donor HLA antibodies (often pre-existing or developing post-transplant) attacking donor endothelial cells. The clinical picture (diarrhea, rash, jaundice) and biopsy findings (bile duct injury, hepatocyte apoptosis, spongiosis) are more typical of cellular GVHD, and the flow cytometry points to T cell involvement, not antibody production.
- (D): Recipient NK cells recognizing and killing donor cells lacking inhibitory signals from recipient HLA-C molecules. Incorrect. This describes a mechanism of graft rejection, where recipient NK cells recognize donor cells as foreign due to missing self-HLA ligands (like HLA-C or HLA-E) on the donor cells. This is less common in HSCT, especially when the recipient is the one being attacked (GVHD). Furthermore, the flow cytometry specifically identifies donor T cells as the effector cells.
- (E): Donor dendritic cells presenting recipient self-antigens to recipient T cells, leading to autoimmune-like liver injury. Incorrect. This describes a mechanism potentially involved in chronic GVHD or autoimmune phenomena post-transplant, but it doesn't fit the acute presentation and the specific finding of donor T cells recognizing minor histocompatibility antigens presented by HLA-C. The primary mechanism in acute GVHD is donor T cells recognizing recipient antigens.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | Correct Answer (B)                                                                                                                               | Distractor (A)                                                                                                                               | Distractor (C)                                                                                                                               | Distractor (D)                                                                                                                               | Distractor (E)                                                                                                                               |
| :-------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Mechanism**               | Donor T cells recognize recipient minor Ags presented by donor HLA-C (Direct Alloreactivity)                                                        | Donor T cells recognize recipient HLA-A (Direct Alloreactivity)                                                                                | Recipient B cells make anti-donor HLA antibodies (Antibody-Mediated Rejection)                                                                 | Recipient NK cells kill donor cells (Graft Rejection)                                                                                           | Donor DCs present recipient self-Ags to recipient T cells (Autoimmune-like)                                                                    |
| **Target Antigen**           | Recipient Minor Histocompatibility Antigen (presented by HLA-C)                                                                                 | Recipient HLA-A                                                                                                                               | Donor HLA-C                                                                                                                               | Donor HLA-C (missing self)                                                                                                                      | Recipient Self-Antigens                                                                                                                         |
| **Effector Cell**           | Donor CD8+ T cells                                                                                                                               | Donor T cells                                                                                                                               | Recipient B cells                                                                                                                             | Recipient NK cells                                                                                                                             | Recipient T cells                                                                                                                             |
| **HLA Mismatch**            | HLA-C (C*04:01 vs C*04:02)                                                                                                                         | HLA-A (Mismatch implied, but not present in vignette)                                                                                           | HLA-C (Antibody target)                                                                                                                         | HLA-C (Missing self)                                                                                                                             | N/A                                                                                                                                             |
| **Clinical Picture**         | Acute GVHD (Diarrhea, Rash, Jaundice)                                                                                                             | Acute GVHD (Diarrhea, Rash, Jaundice)                                                                                                             | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (often vasculitis, graft dysfunction)                                                                               | Graft Rejection (less common in HSCT, different clinical picture)                                                                                | Chronic GVHD/Autoimmunity (often later onset, different organ involvement)                                                                     |
| **Flow Cytometry Finding** | Donor CD8+ T cells specific for minor Ag presented by HLA-C                                                                                       | N/A                                                                                                                                             | N/A                                                                                                                                             | N/A                                                                                                                                             | N/A                                                                                                                                             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute GVHD is mediated by donor T cells recognizing recipient antigens as foreign. Minor histocompatibility antigens, presented by HLA molecules (often HLA-C, HLA-E, or HLA-G), are key triggers, especially when major HLA matching is complete. Flow cytometry identifying donor T cell specificity is diagnostic. Differentiate GVHD from rejection (recipient attacking donor) and antibody-mediated rejection (B cells/antibodies).

<a id='question-082'></a>

### Question 082: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 32 (fashion.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanisms of acute rejection in organ transplantation, specifically T-cell mediated rejection involving direct allorecognition and the role of donor MHC molecules on recipient dendritic cells. (理解器官移植中急性排斥反应的机制，特别是涉及直接异体识别的T细胞介导的排斥反应以及供体MHC分子在受体树突状细胞上的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with an A blood type, while the recipient is blood type O. The patient is immediately started on standard immunosuppression including a calcineurin inhibitor (cyclosporine), an antiproliferative agent (mycophenolate mofetil), and corticosteroids (prednisone). Three weeks post-transplant, the patient develops fever, flank pain, and decreased urine output. Serum creatinine rises from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL. A renal biopsy is performed, revealing diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, edema, and evidence of endothelialitis in peritubular capillaries. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood reveals a significant increase in CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes. Further investigation reveals that the recipient's dendritic cells present donor MHC class I molecules on their surface. Which of the following mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the acute rejection observed in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection involving pre-formed anti-donor ABO antibodies leading to complement activation and endothelial damage.
(B) Antibody-mediated rejection involving de novo anti-donor HLA antibodies binding to endothelial cells and activating complement.
(C) T-cell mediated rejection involving direct allorecognition of donor MHC class I molecules presented by recipient dendritic cells, leading to cytotoxic T lymphocyte activation.
(D) T-cell mediated rejection involving indirect allorecognition of donor MHC molecules presented by recipient dendritic cells after processing donor antigens in the context of self-MHC.
(E) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes mediated by cytokines like TGF-β and PDGF released by macrophages.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, ESRD due to diabetic nephropathy, receives kidney transplant. This sets the stage for potential transplant rejection.
- **ABO Incompatibility:** Donor is A, recipient is O. This is a major ABO incompatibility, which typically leads to hyperacute rejection if pre-formed antibodies are present, or acute antibody-mediated rejection if antibodies develop post-transplant. However, the vignette describes features more consistent with T-cell mediated rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard triple therapy (calcineurin inhibitor, antiproliferative, steroids) is initiated, suggesting an attempt to prevent rejection.
- **Timeline:** Rejection occurs 3 weeks post-transplant, typical for acute rejection (either T-cell mediated or antibody-mediated).
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, rising creatinine indicate graft dysfunction.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, edema, endothelialitis. These are classic signs of acute rejection, particularly T-cell mediated rejection. Endothelialitis is a key feature.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries suggests complement activation, which can occur in both antibody-mediated rejection and T-cell mediated rejection (TCM) with endothelialitis.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes points towards a cell-mediated immune response targeting graft cells.
- **Dendritic Cell Presentation:** Recipient dendritic cells present donor MHC class I molecules. This is the hallmark of *direct* allorecognition.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Rejection:** Occurs within days to months post-transplant. Can be T-cell mediated (TCMR) or antibody-mediated (ABMR).
- **TCMR:** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor MHC molecules as foreign.
    - **Direct Pathway:** Recipient T cells (especially CD8+ CTLs) directly recognize intact donor MHC molecules (MHC class I and II) presented on donor antigen-presenting cells (APCs) like dendritic cells that have migrated from the graft to recipient lymphoid organs, or on graft endothelial cells. This is a rapid and potent response. The vignette explicitly states recipient dendritic cells present donor MHC class I.
    - **Indirect Pathway:** Recipient APCs process donor MHC molecules (like any other foreign protein) and present peptides derived from donor MHC molecules on self-MHC molecules (MHC class II) to recipient T helper cells. This pathway is slower and typically occurs later or in chronic rejection.
- **ABMR:** Mediated by antibodies (pre-formed or de novo) against donor HLA antigens, particularly HLA class I expressed on endothelial cells. Antibody binding activates complement (leading to C4d deposition) and recruits inflammatory cells, causing endothelial damage (endothelialitis) and thrombosis.
- **Vignette Analysis:** The biopsy shows lymphocytic infiltrates (T cells), endothelialitis, C4d deposition, and increased CD8+ T cells. The key finding is that recipient dendritic cells present *donor MHC class I molecules*. This directly points to the *direct pathway* of T-cell mediated rejection. While C4d deposition is often associated with ABMR, it can also be seen in TCMR, especially when endothelialitis is present, due to complement activation by T-cell products or other inflammatory mediators. The ABO incompatibility (A donor to O recipient) could potentially lead to ABMR if anti-A antibodies were present, but the dominant features described (lymphocytic infiltrate, endothelialitis, CD8+ T cell increase, direct presentation of donor MHC) strongly favor TCMR via the direct pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-mediated rejection involving pre-formed anti-donor ABO antibodies. While ABO incompatibility exists, hyperacute rejection (mediated by pre-formed antibodies) occurs within minutes to hours, not weeks. The vignette describes acute rejection at 3 weeks. Also, the primary histological features described (lymphocytic infiltrate, endothelialitis) are more typical of TCMR, although ABMR can also cause endothelialitis. The key clue against this is the description of direct presentation of donor MHC by recipient dendritic cells, which is specific to TCMR.
- (B): Antibody-mediated rejection involving de novo anti-donor HLA antibodies. This is a possible cause of acute rejection, often presenting similarly with endothelialitis and C4d deposition. However, the vignette specifically highlights the *direct presentation of donor MHC class I molecules by recipient dendritic cells*, which is the defining characteristic of the direct pathway of TCMR, not ABMR. ABMR involves antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens on graft cells.
- (D): T-cell mediated rejection involving indirect allorecognition. This pathway involves recipient APCs processing donor MHC molecules and presenting peptides on self-MHC class II molecules to recipient T helper cells. This is a slower process and less common in acute rejection compared to the direct pathway. The vignette explicitly describes direct presentation of intact donor MHC class I molecules, ruling out the indirect pathway as the *most direct* mechanism.
- (E): Chronic rejection. This typically occurs months to years post-transplant and is characterized by fibrosis, vascular changes (e.g., transplant glomerulopathy, chronic interstitial fibrosis), and gradual loss of graft function. The vignette describes acute rejection occurring at 3 weeks with inflammatory features, not chronic fibrotic changes.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Direct TCMR (Correct Answer)                                  | Indirect TCMR (Distractor D)                               | ABMR (Distractor B)                                     | Hyperacute Rejection (Distractor A)                     | Chronic Rejection (Distractor E)                        |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------ | :--------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Timing**              | Days to weeks (Acute)                                         | Weeks to months (Subacute/Chronic)                         | Days to weeks (Acute)                                   | Minutes to hours                                        | Months to years                                         |
| **Mechanism**           | T cells recognize intact donor MHC on donor APCs/endothelial cells | T cells recognize processed donor MHC peptides on self-MHC | Antibodies bind donor HLA, activate complement/ADCC     | Pre-formed antibodies bind donor ABO/HLA antigens       | Fibrosis, vascular changes, cytokine-mediated damage    |
| **Key Cells**           | CD8+ CTLs, CD4+ T cells                                       | CD4+ T cells                                               | B cells, Antibodies, Complement, NK cells                | Antibodies, Complement                                   | Macrophages, Fibroblasts, T cells                       |
| **Histology**           | Lymphocytic infiltrate, endothelialitis, edema                 | Lymphocytic infiltrate, less prominent endothelialitis      | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, thrombi, inflammation | Thrombosis, endothelial damage, inflammation            | Fibrosis, vascular thickening, interstitial inflammation |
| **MHC Presentation**    | Direct presentation of donor MHC by donor/recipient APCs       | Indirect presentation of donor MHC peptides by recipient APCs | N/A (Antibody-mediated)                                 | N/A (Antibody-mediated)                                 | N/A                                                     |
| **Vignette Clue**       | Recipient DCs present donor MHC class I                       | N/A                                                        | N/A                                                     | N/A                                                     | N/A                                                     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) is a major cause of graft loss. The direct pathway, characterized by T cells recognizing intact donor MHC molecules presented by donor or recipient APCs, is a rapid and potent mechanism. Key histological features include lymphocytic infiltrates and endothelialitis. While C4d deposition is more classically associated with antibody-mediated rejection (ABMR), it can occur in TCMR, especially with endothelialitis. Differentiating TCMR from ABMR is crucial for management. Remember that direct allorecognition involves the presentation of intact donor MHC molecules, distinguishing it from the indirect pathway where donor MHC peptides are presented on self-MHC.

<a id='question-083'></a>

### Question 083: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 33 (individual)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanism of action of calcineurin inhibitors (cyclosporine, tacrolimus) in preventing T-cell activation following transplantation, specifically their effect on intracellular signaling pathways and cytokine production. (理解钙调神经磷酸酶抑制剂（环孢素、他克莫司）在移植后预防T细胞活化的作用机制，特别是它们对细胞内信号通路和细胞因子产生的影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) score is 10%, indicating low sensitization. Post-transplant, he is started on immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops acute rejection, characterized by fever, flank pain, and elevated serum creatinine (from a baseline of 1.5 mg/dL to 3.0 mg/dL). A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates. His tacrolimus trough level is found to be subtherapeutic (target range 8-12 ng/mL, patient level 5 ng/mL). The transplant team increases the tacrolimus dose. Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains how tacrolimus prevents acute T-cell mediated rejection in this patient?

(A) Inhibition of IL-2 receptor signaling on T cells.
(B) Prevention of T-cell receptor (TCR) complex formation on the T-cell surface.
(C) Blockade of the interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules on antigen-presenting cells (APCs).
(D) Inhibition of calcineurin, leading to decreased transcription of IL-2 and other cytokines.
(E) Induction of apoptosis in activated T cells via the Fas/FasL pathway.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the context for immunosuppression and potential rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone. Tacrolimus is a key immunosuppressant, specifically a calcineurin inhibitor.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Acute rejection (fever, flank pain, rising creatinine) 3 weeks post-transplant. This indicates T-cell mediated rejection, a common complication.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates. Confirms acute rejection.
- **Tacrolimus Level:** Subtherapeutic (5 ng/mL vs. target 8-12 ng/mL). Suggests inadequate drug effect contributing to rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism of action of tacrolimus in preventing T-cell mediated rejection. This directly tests knowledge of tacrolimus pharmacology and immunology.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T-cell Activation:** T-cell activation requires two signals:
    1.  Signal 1: TCR engagement with peptide-MHC complex on APCs.
    2.  Signal 2: Co-stimulation, typically CD28 on T cells binding to B7 (CD80/CD86) on APCs.
- **Intracellular Signaling:** Signal 1 (TCR engagement) leads to activation of intracellular tyrosine kinases (like Lck), phosphorylation of immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motifs (ITAMs) in the TCR complex, and recruitment of ZAP-70. This initiates a signaling cascade involving LAT, SLP-76, and ultimately leads to the activation of NFAT (Nuclear Factor of Activated T-cells).
- **Calcineurin's Role:** Calcineurin is a calcium-dependent phosphatase (specifically, a serine/threonine phosphatase). Upon T-cell activation (Signal 1), intracellular calcium levels rise, activating calcineurin.
- **NFAT Activation:** Activated calcineurin dephosphorylates NFAT. This dephosphorylation allows NFAT to translocate from the cytoplasm into the nucleus.
- **Cytokine Transcription:** In the nucleus, NFAT binds to promoter regions of genes encoding key T-cell cytokines, most notably IL-2, but also IL-4, IFN-γ, and TNF-α. This binding promotes the transcription of these cytokines.
- **Tacrolimus Mechanism:** Tacrolimus (and cyclosporine) binds to intracellular immunophilin proteins (FKBP12 for tacrolimus, cyclophilin for cyclosporine). This complex then binds to and inhibits calcineurin.
- **Consequence of Tacrolimus:** By inhibiting calcineurin, tacrolimus prevents the dephosphorylation and nuclear translocation of NFAT. This blocks the transcription of IL-2 and other critical T-cell cytokines. IL-2 is crucial for T-cell proliferation and differentiation, thus inhibiting its production suppresses the T-cell response responsible for rejection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Inhibition of IL-2 receptor signaling on T cells. This is the mechanism of action of IL-2 receptor antagonists like basiliximab and daclizumab. These are monoclonal antibodies that block the binding of IL-2 to its receptor (CD25, the alpha chain), preventing downstream signaling (JAK/STAT pathway). Tacrolimus acts upstream by preventing IL-2 production.
- (B) Prevention of T-cell receptor (TCR) complex formation on the T-cell surface. TCR complex formation is essential for T-cell recognition and activation. Agents that interfere with this process are generally not used clinically as immunosuppressants due to broad effects and potential toxicity. Tacrolimus does not affect TCR complex formation; it acts downstream of TCR signaling.
- (C) Blockade of the interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules on antigen-presenting cells (APCs). This is the mechanism of action of CTLA4-Ig (abatacept) and anti-CD28 antibodies (teplizumab). These agents interfere with the crucial co-stimulatory signal required for full T-cell activation. Tacrolimus does not affect the CD28-B7 interaction.
- (E) Induction of apoptosis in activated T cells via the Fas/FasL pathway. While apoptosis is important in regulating immune responses, and some immunosuppressants might indirectly influence it, the primary mechanism of tacrolimus is not direct induction of apoptosis via Fas/FasL. Agents like alemtuzumab (anti-CD52) induce apoptosis, but through a different pathway.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Tacrolimus (Calcineurin Inhibitor) | Basiliximab/Daclizumab (IL-2 Receptor Ab) | Abatacept (CTLA4-Ig) | Anti-CD28 Ab | Alemtuzumab (Anti-CD52 Ab) |
| :---------------------- | :--------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------- | :------------------ | :----------- | :------------------------ |
| **Target**              | Calcineurin                        | IL-2 Receptor (CD25)                      | CD28-B7 Interaction | CD28         | CD52 (on lymphocytes)     |
| **Mechanism**           | Inhibits NFAT translocation, ↓ IL-2 transcription | Blocks IL-2 binding, ↓ IL-2 signaling | Blocks CD28 co-stimulation | Blocks CD28 co-stimulation | Induces lymphocyte apoptosis |
| **Effect on T-cell Activation** | Blocks cytokine production (Signal 2) | Blocks cytokine signaling (Signal 2) | Blocks co-stimulation (Signal 2) | Blocks co-stimulation (Signal 2) | Depletes lymphocytes        |
| **Primary Use**         | Prevent/treat rejection           | Prevent acute rejection                   | Prevent rejection   | Prevent rejection | Lymphocyte depletion (e.g., MS, CLL) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Tacrolimus prevents T-cell activation by inhibiting calcineurin, which blocks NFAT nuclear translocation and subsequent IL-2 transcription. This mechanism differs from blocking IL-2 signaling (basiliximab), blocking co-stimulation (abatacept, anti-CD28), or inducing apoptosis (alemtuzumab). Understanding the specific intracellular pathway targeted by different immunosuppressants is crucial for managing transplant patients and understanding adverse effects.

<a id='question-084'></a>

### Question 084: Transplantation: Part 2 Focus 34 (inherits)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanism of inheritance of alloantigen recognition in transplantation, specifically how T cells recognize foreign MHC molecules presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs) and the role of indirect allorecognition in graft rejection. (理解移植中alloantigen识别的遗传机制，特别是T细胞如何识别由受体抗原提呈细胞(APCs)呈递的异体MHC分子，以及间接同源识别在移植排斥中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from his HLA-A*02:01, HLA-B*15:02, HLA-DRB1*03:01 positive deceased donor. The patient is HLA-A*02:01, HLA-B*15:02, HLA-DRB1*03:01 negative. He is started on standard immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Six months post-transplant, the patient develops acute graft dysfunction, characterized by rising serum creatinine and proteinuria. A kidney biopsy reveals interstitial inflammation with mononuclear cell infiltrates and evidence of acute tubular injury. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows an increase in CD4+ T cells reactive to donor-derived MHC class II molecules. Further investigation reveals that these CD4+ T cells recognize peptides derived from donor MHC class II molecules presented by the patient's own APCs. Which of the following mechanisms best explains the activation of these CD4+ T cells leading to graft rejection?

(A) Direct allorecognition, where recipient T cells recognize intact donor MHC molecules on donor APCs that migrated into the recipient's circulation.
(B) Indirect allorecognition, where recipient APCs process donor MHC molecules and present peptides derived from them on recipient MHC class II molecules to recipient T cells.
(C) Recognition of recipient MHC molecules by donor T cells that survived the transplantation procedure and infiltrated the graft.
(D) Antibody-mediated rejection, where pre-formed recipient antibodies against donor MHC molecules activate complement and cause endothelial injury.
(E) Recognition of non-MHC antigens (minor histocompatibility antigens) on donor cells by recipient T cells.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient.
- **HLA Mismatch:** Donor is HLA-A*02:01, B*15:02, DRB1*03:01 positive; Recipient is HLA-A*02:01, B*15:02, DRB1*03:01 negative. This indicates a fully mismatched kidney transplant regarding these major HLA loci.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Acute graft dysfunction (rising creatinine, proteinuria) 6 months post-transplant, suggesting acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial inflammation with mononuclear cell infiltrates and acute tubular injury, consistent with cellular rejection.
- **Immunological Findings:** Increased CD4+ T cells reactive to donor-derived MHC class II molecules. Crucially, these T cells recognize peptides derived from donor MHC class II molecules presented by the *patient's own APCs*.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the activation of these CD4+ T cells.

The key clue is that the recipient's T cells recognize *peptides derived from donor MHC molecules* presented by the *recipient's own APCs* on recipient MHC class II molecules. This is the definition of indirect allorecognition.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Allorecognition Pathways:** In transplantation, T cell-mediated rejection occurs via two main pathways: direct and indirect allorecognition.
    - **Direct Allorecognition:** Recipient T cells recognize intact, foreign MHC molecules (MHC class I or II) expressed on the surface of donor APCs (e.g., dendritic cells) that have migrated from the graft into the recipient's secondary lymphoid organs or local tissues. This pathway is considered more potent and is primarily responsible for acute cellular rejection. It requires the donor APCs to survive and migrate. The T cell receptor (TCR) directly binds to the foreign MHC molecule presenting a self-peptide or a foreign peptide.
    - **Indirect Allorecognition:** Recipient APCs process donor MHC molecules (or other donor proteins) that are released from the graft (e.g., due to cell damage or apoptosis). These donor MHC molecules are degraded into peptides, which are then loaded onto the recipient APCs' own MHC class II molecules and presented to recipient CD4+ T cells. The recipient T cells recognize these donor-derived peptides presented by self-MHC class II. This pathway is considered less potent than direct recognition but is important in chronic rejection and can contribute to acute rejection, especially in the context of antibody-mediated rejection or when direct recognition is impaired. It resembles the response to foreign antigens from pathogens.
- **CD4+ T Cell Activation:** CD4+ T cells (helper T cells) recognize peptides presented on MHC class II molecules. In the context of transplantation, they can be activated by either direct or indirect allorecognition.
- **Vignette Specifics:** The vignette explicitly states that the recipient's CD4+ T cells recognize *peptides derived from donor MHC class II molecules* presented by the *patient's own APCs*. This precisely describes the mechanism of indirect allorecognition.

##### 3. Role of Indirect Allorecognition in Graft Rejection
- Indirect allorecognition involves the presentation of donor MHC peptides by recipient APCs on self-MHC class II molecules to recipient CD4+ T cells.
- This pathway is thought to be important in chronic rejection and in situations where direct allorecognition is less effective or absent (e.g., due to immunosuppression targeting donor APCs or specific types of graft damage).
- While direct allorecognition is often considered the primary driver of acute cellular rejection, indirect allorecognition can also contribute, especially as the rejection process evolves or in specific clinical scenarios.
- The vignette describes a scenario consistent with indirect allorecognition being the mechanism responsible for activating the observed CD4+ T cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Direct allorecognition** involves recipient T cells recognizing *intact* foreign MHC molecules on *donor* APCs. The vignette specifies that the recipient T cells recognize *peptides derived from* donor MHC molecules presented by *recipient* APCs. This is incorrect. Direct recognition is a major pathway, but not the one described here.
- (C): **Recognition of recipient MHC molecules by donor T cells** is not relevant to graft rejection by the recipient's immune system. Donor T cells are typically eliminated or suppressed post-transplant. This option describes a hypothetical scenario, not the mechanism of rejection in this patient.
- (D): **Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR)** involves antibodies against donor HLA antigens. While AMR can occur, the vignette describes T cell activation recognizing processed peptides presented by APCs, which is characteristic of cellular rejection, not primarily AMR. Although T cells play a role in AMR, the described mechanism (CD4+ T cells recognizing donor MHC peptides on recipient APCs) is specifically indirect allorecognition, a form of cellular rejection.
- (E): **Recognition of non-MHC antigens (minor histocompatibility antigens)** is another mechanism of graft rejection. Recipient T cells recognize peptides derived from these minor antigens presented by recipient APCs. While this can occur, the vignette specifically mentions recognition of *donor MHC class II* peptides, pointing towards allorecognition involving the major MHC molecules, not minor histocompatibility antigens.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Direct Allorecognition                                  | Indirect Allorecognition                               | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR)                     | Minor Histocompatibility Antigen Recognition          |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------ | :----------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- |
| **Target Antigen**       | Intact foreign MHC molecules (Class I or II)           | Peptides derived from foreign MHC molecules             | Donor HLA antigens (recognized by antibodies)          | Peptides derived from non-MHC antigens                 |
| **Presenting Cell**      | Donor APCs (e.g., dendritic cells)                     | Recipient APCs                                          | Endothelial cells (target), recipient APCs (involved) | Recipient APCs                                          |
| **T Cell Receptor (TCR)** | Binds directly to foreign MHC + peptide                | Binds to self-MHC + donor MHC-derived peptide           | N/A (primarily antibody-mediated)                      | Binds to self-MHC + minor antigen-derived peptide     |
| **Primary Cell Type**    | CD8+ T cells (for MHC I), CD4+ T cells (for MHC II)    | CD4+ T cells                                            | B cells (producing antibodies), NK cells, macrophages | CD8+ or CD4+ T cells                                   |
| **Relevance**            | Acute cellular rejection (major pathway)               | Chronic rejection, contributes to acute rejection        | Acute or chronic rejection, vascular injury            | Can contribute to acute or chronic rejection           |
| **Vignette Match**       | No (requires intact donor MHC on donor APCs)           | Yes (recipient APCs present donor MHC peptides)         | No (describes T cell recognition of peptides)          | No (describes recognition of donor MHC peptides)       |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The key distinction between direct and indirect allorecognition lies in whether recipient T cells recognize intact foreign MHC molecules on donor APCs (direct) or processed peptides derived from donor MHC molecules presented by recipient APCs on self-MHC (indirect). The vignette clearly describes indirect allorecognition. Differentiate this from direct allorecognition (the main mechanism of acute cellular rejection) and antibody-mediated rejection (involving antibodies against donor HLA). Remember that indirect allorecognition resembles the response to foreign antigens from pathogens.
